## STOPS

### STOP-1 -- T-A2's A2-b / A2-c drive does not reach the site it tests (a ruling fact that reads against the code; RD's table rows)

**What the plan says.** T-A2 A2-b: "FILLED, `price=10.0`, `executions=None`, `quantity=0.5`" -> pre-fix `kind="populated"`, `shares == 0`. A2-c: the same with `quantity=0.0` ("admitted at `models.py:336`"). RD's supersession (ledger, R1 gate): "[0,1) IS reachable, via the so.quantity fallback (0 is admitted at :336) and via fractional legs."

**What the code does (by read AND by execution on the unmodified tree).** At the entry site, `_compute_execution_price(chosen)` (`schwab_reconciliation.py:464-466`) returns `None` whenever `executions` is `None` or empty -- which is EXACTLY when `_resolve_match_quantity` falls back to `so.quantity` (`:539-542`). `entry_auto_fill.py:431-444` returns `kind="empty"` ("lacks execution-grain price") on a `None` price BEFORE `:445-446` is reached. So the `so.quantity` fallback path NEVER reaches `int(quantity)` at the entry site. Probe (scratch script, the real resolver, the three Schwab seams stubbed as the sibling test module stubs them):

```
plan drive executions=None quantity 0.5 -> ('empty', None, 'Schwab BUY fill for AAPL lacks execution-grain price; please enter manually.')
plan drive executions=None quantity 0.0 -> ('empty', None, 'Schwab BUY fill for AAPL lacks execution-grain price; please enter manually.')
fractional LEG 0.5 -> ('populated', 0, None)
```

**Consequence.** As written, A2-b and A2-c pass on BOTH trees (the price branch pre-empts the guard) -- non-discriminating, a defect by the plan's own rule. At the entry site `[0,1)` is reachable ONLY through fractional LEGS (sum of legs each `> 0`, `models.py:216`); a sum of positive legs can never be `0.0`, so A2-c's `0.0` is unreachable at the entry site by any validated path.

**Why this is a STOP and not an encoded correction.** The dispatch: "A change to any T-A1, T-A2 or T-A3 table row DOES re-open [RD's read], so make none." Correcting the drive IS a table-row change.

**Both branches, ruler RD:**
- **(a) CORRECT THE DRIVE:** A2-b drives ONE execution leg of `quantity=0.5`, `price=10.0` (pre-fix, measured: `populated`, `shares == 0`; post-fix: `empty`, the sub-one-share advisory). A2-c becomes a GUARD TEST planted at the resolver's return (`_resolve_match_quantity` -> `0.0`, counting wrapper asserted >= 1; labelled unreachable on validated models, like A2-a/A2-d), pre-fix `populated`/`shares==0`, post-fix `empty`/sub-one-share. Optionally keep the plan's `executions=None` drives as CONTROLS pinning that the price branch pre-empts (pass on both trees, labelled so). The fix code is unchanged.
- **(b) KEEP THE ROWS AS WRITTEN** and relabel A2-b/A2-c as controls (they pass on both trees); the `[0,1)` refusal at the entry site would then have NO discriminator in this arc. Not recommended: C1-27's real content (RD's own words) would ship untested.

The exit side (T-A3) is NOT affected: `_build_candidate` collects every reason, and T-A3's rows do not prescribe `executions=None` (A3-d's `0.9` is driven by one fractional leg, so the reasons are `("sub_one_share_quantity",)` alone).

**State at the stop:** T-A1 committed (`1961f0ad`); T-A2 not started in code; nothing uncommitted.

#### STOP-1 -- RULED by RD (2026-09-25 15:08Z): (a) CORRECT THE DRIVE; A2-b one leg, A2-c guard, A2-b'/A2-c' controls REQUIRED; T-A2 RE-CLEARED

Author: RD (seat `swing-rd-20260924-2100`). Courier: the orchestrator. Text of record: role-mail `20260925T150827Z-rd-stop-1-ruling-a-correct-the-drive-a2-b-o.md`. The literal block quote follows, the message body verbatim:

> STOP-1 RULING (RD, sole ruler): (a) -- CORRECT THE DRIVE, NOT THE FIX -- with the controls made REQUIRED, not optional. The fact is verified at this seat before ruling: _compute_execution_price returns None when executions is None or empty (schwab_reconciliation.py:446-453, the docstring's own first bullet), and entry_auto_fill.py:431-444 returns kind="empty" on a None price BEFORE :445-446 is reached. So the A2-b/A2-c drives as I cleared them never reach the guard: they pass on both trees. The cell's measurement is the instrument; my clearance of those two rows rested on a drive I did not compute under the naive substitute, which is the rule I wrote on 2026-09-08 and did not apply here.
>
> THE CORRECTION TO MY OWN SUPERSESSION (item 3, R1 gate), stated so the ledger carries it beside the original: "[0,1) IS reachable via the so.quantity fallback" is FALSE AT THE ENTRY SITE. The fallback is only taken when executions is None or empty, and on that same condition the price branch pre-empts the quantity guard. At the entry site [0,1) is reachable ONLY through fractional legs (each leg > 0 and finite, models.py:216, so a leg sum is > 0 and can sit in (0,1)); exact 0.0 is reachable by NO validated path. At the EXIT site the fallback half still holds: _build_candidate derives price and quantity independently and collects every reason (exit_auto_fill.py:1192-1200 on this tree), so executions=None with so.quantity in [0,1) yields ("no_execution_price", "sub_one_share_quantity") there. Two sites, two reachability statements; I had written one for both.
>
> THE ROWS AS RULED:
> - A2-b: ONE execution leg, quantity=0.5, price=10.0 (a valid SchwabExecutionLeg). Pre-fix: populated, shares == 0 (the cell's measured ('populated', 0, None) IS the C1-27 defect, on the real path). Post-fix: kind="empty", advisory contains "below one whole share", NOT "non-finite".
> - A2-c: a GUARD TEST planted at swing.trades.entry_auto_fill._resolve_match_quantity returning 0.0, counting wrapper asserted called >= 1 (R4-01), labelled "unreachable on validated models" exactly as A2-a and A2-d are. Pre-fix: populated, shares == 0. Post-fix: empty, sub-one-share advisory.
> - A2-b' and A2-c' (REQUIRED, the former drives kept as CONTROLS): executions=None with quantity=0.5 and with quantity=0.0 -> kind="empty" with the "lacks execution-grain price" advisory on BOTH trees, and the advisory does NOT contain "below one whole share". These pin the pre-emption ORDER that made the original rows vacuous: if a later arc moves the price branch below the quantity guard, the control goes red and says why. A control that pins the ordering is what turns this STOP into a test instead of a memory.
> - A2-a, A2-a', A2-d, A2-e, A2-f: UNCHANGED.
>
> WHY (a) AND NOT (b): (b) leaves C1-27's real content with no discriminator in the arc that fixes it -- a fix with no RED is a fix nobody can tell is present, and the whole 22-F plan rests on RED-first. The fix code is unaffected, so (a) costs two drives and two controls.
>
> RE-OPEN AND RE-CLEARANCE, by my own terms: a table-row change re-opens my item-2 read. It is RE-CLEARED HERE on T-A2 as ruled above; the clearance attaches to the ledger transcription of these rows and to the RED output of A2-b and A2-c quoted in the T-A2 commit body (the plan's own RED-first rule, which is what this STOP was). T-A1, T-A3 and A6-f are untouched and their clearance stands. Nothing else re-opens.
>
> For the phase record: this is the third correction of one sentence of mine in one arc (the citation, the reachability, now the drive), each caught by a seat that RAN the thing -- the census's grep, the orchestrator's read, the cell's measurement. Rulings stated as semantics survived every time; every stated fact about a path I had not executed did not.

**Encoding obligations (orchestrator, no ruling of its own):** the T-A2 rows are executed AS RULED above; the plan's T-A2 table is superseded for A2-b/A2-c by this block, and A2-b'/A2-c' are added. ### STOP-2 -- T-D5 step 0 FAILED: `swing config policy import-from-toml` does NOT repair a DB with no active policy (CHARC condition 2; D35 class)

**Run (temp DB only, CliRunner, `USERPROFILE`/`HOME` monkeypatched; transcript `red/T-D5-step0.txt`):** `db-migrate` -> `risk_policy` = `[(1, 1)]`; a raw `UPDATE risk_policy SET is_active = 0` was accepted -> `[(1, 0)]`; `config policy import-from-toml --field capital_floor_constant_dollars` -> **exit 1, `RuntimeError: No active risk_policy row — schema corrupted or seed missing`** (uncaught: empty output, the exception is the result); the same for `--field max_concurrent_positions`; afterwards active rows = **0**. No repair.

**Mechanism (read):** `policy_import_from_toml` (`swing/cli_config.py:486-556`) calls `supersede_active_policy` (`swing/trades/risk_policy.py:81`), which SELECTs the active row and raises `RuntimeError("No active risk_policy row ...")` when there is none (`:135-142`). It catches only `CallerHeldTransactionError`/`ValueError`, so the RuntimeError escapes as a traceback. **Every** operator writer of `risk_policy` goes through `supersede_active_policy`: CLI `config set`/`config reset`/`policy set`/`import-from-toml` (`cli_config.py:175`, `:253`, `:471`, `:540`) and web `POST /config`, `POST /config/reset/{field}` (`routes/config.py:171`, `:247`). Search: `grep -rn "supersede_active_policy(\|INSERT INTO risk_policy" swing/`. The one function that CREATES an active row when none exists is `seed_initial_policy` (`trades/risk_policy.py:384`, "Idempotent seed fall-back ... repair paths"). It has **no caller in swing/**, only its own module docstring (`:22`). The single-fire v17 ratification cannot re-fire. So the operator has **no command** that repairs this state, and the refusal the plan specifies ("Run: swing config policy import-from-toml") would name an action that fails. (Incidental: the plan's text also omits the REQUIRED `--field`.)

**Side observation (not fixed; outside the census rows as read):** `import-from-toml` on a no-active-policy DB is itself a class-1 escape (an untyped `RuntimeError` traceback). Its siblings `config set`/`config reset`/`policy set` likely escape the same way through `supersede_active_policy`; this was not driven.

**Nothing written for T-D5.** No handler, no closure test. Returned to the orchestrator for CHARC. Branches as the cell sees them (ruler: **CHARC**, F-F4 (3) / R45 are his):
- **(a) Name the truth, no command.** The 503 page and the S-CLICK text state the state ("no active risk_policy row; the policy table needs repair (restore from backup, or re-seed)") and name no command, since none works. Handler + closure as planned; only the message changes.
- **(b) Give the operator a working repair.** Expose `seed_initial_policy` as a CLI command (e.g. `swing config policy seed`) in `cli_config.py` (in the envelope, not carve-out-governed), then name THAT command in the refusal. This is new operator behaviour beyond the census rows (section 0.3's bound), so it needs a ruling. Step 0 would be re-run against the new command.
- **(c) Make the named command work.** Teach `supersede_active_policy` / `import-from-toml` to seed when no row is active. This changes `trades/risk_policy.py` semantics on a money-bearing table. It is the widest of the three.

### STOP-3 -- the STOP-2 ruling's six-writer discriminator reads against the code for FOUR of the six writers (a ruling that reads two ways; ruler: CHARC)

**Measured (temp DB, raw `UPDATE risk_policy SET is_active = 0`, CliRunner / TestClient, `USERPROFILE`+`HOME` monkeypatched, no code change; transcript `red/T-D5-six-writers.txt`, scratch tests deleted):**
- CLI `config set account.risk_equity_floor 8500.0 --force` -> **exit 0**; output `WARNING: risk_policy cascade failed (No active risk_policy row -- ...); override written but risk_policy NOT updated. Run `swing config policy import-from-toml --field capital_floor_constant_dollars` to reconcile.`; `user-config.toml` now holds `risk_equity_floor = 8500.0`; active rows 0.
- CLI `config reset account.risk_equity_floor` -> **exit 0**; the same WARNING shape naming `import-from-toml`; the override IS deleted; active rows 0.
- CLI `config policy set --field max_concurrent_positions --value 5` -> **exit 1, uncaught `RuntimeError`** (empty output); active rows 0. (`import-from-toml`: the same, `red/T-D5-step0.txt`.)
- web `POST /config` (risk_equity_floor 8500, force) -> **204, `HX-Redirect: /config?saved=1`**; the override IS written; active rows 0 (the failure is `log.exception`-ed with the `import-from-toml` hint).
- web `POST /config/reset/account.risk_equity_floor` -> **204, `HX-Redirect: /config?saved=1`**; the override IS deleted; active rows 0.

**Handler tuples READ (ruling item 4's "none narrows" -- CONFIRMED):** `cli_config.py:175` (`config_set`) and `:253` (`config_reset`): the `supersede_active_policy` call sits in a `try` whose handler is `except Exception as exc` (WARNING echo, exit 0); the inner `except NoActivePolicyError` at `:164`/`:242` wraps ONLY the `get_active_policy` call at `:163`/`:241`, not the supersede. `cli_config.py:471` (`policy_set`) and `:540` (`policy_import_from_toml`): `except (CallerHeldTransactionError, ValueError)` only. `routes/config.py:171` (`config_save`) and `:247` (`config_reset`): `except Exception:` (`log.exception`, fall through to the save redirect); the inner `except NoActivePolicyError` at `:154`/`:236` wraps only `get_active_policy` at `:153`/`:235`. Method: an AST walk of every call of `get_active_policy`/`read_live_policy`/`read_active_policy`/`supersede_active_policy` in `swing/` with the handler names of every enclosing `Try` body (28 sites), then a read of each writer's handler. So the retype of `trades/risk_policy.py:139-141` changes NO writer's behaviour: nothing narrows.

**The conflict.** Ruling item 4: "each of the six writers -> typed refusal (503 / exit 1 with the text above), NOTHING written, and zero active rows AFTERWARD". True as stated for `policy set` and `import-from-toml` only (they escape today; a per-root `ClickException` gives exit 1 with the text; `supersede_active_policy`'s `except Exception: conn.rollback(); raise` at `trades/risk_policy.py:211-213` writes nothing). For the four CASCADE writers it is false against the code on two counts: (1) they CONTAIN the failure by design (exit 0 / 204 redirect, never a 503 or exit 1), and the plan's own D5-k -- which the ruling keeps -- pins `POST /config` with no active policy as NOT 503; (2) "NOTHING written" is false: the `user-config.toml` override is written (or deleted) BEFORE the cascade, deliberately ("Cascade fires AFTER the override write so a cascade failure leaves the cfg-side change committed", `cli_config.py:133-135`, `routes/config.py:125-131`). And their contained text names `swing config policy import-from-toml --field ...` -- on this state, the D35 class the ruling exists to remove.

**Branches (ruler: CHARC):**
- **(i) Encode per the code, and remove the D35 text in the contained branch.** `policy set` + `import-from-toml`: per-root `ClickException` with the ruling text, exit 1, nothing written, zero active rows (as ruled). The four cascade writers keep their containment (exit 0 / 204; the override stays written -- the documented design; D5-k unchanged) but their handler gains `except NoActivePolicyError` BEFORE the `except Exception` (the retype makes it nameable), which prints / logs the ruling text instead of the `import-from-toml` hint; the other failure causes (a lock, a validator) keep today's hint, which is true for them. Discriminators for the four: the ruling text in the WARNING (CLI) / the log record (web), no `swing <command>` in it, `risk_policy` unchanged, zero active rows. Guard-and-handler edits in `cli_config.py` and `routes/config.py` (both in the envelope).
- **(i') As (i) but leave the four cascade writers' text alone** (their hint is true for every other failure cause; the no-active-policy case would keep naming a command that fails). Narrowest; leaves the D35 text live on four surfaces.
- **(ii) The literal ruling: the four cascade writers REFUSE (exit 1 / 503) with nothing written.** Requires re-ordering override-then-cascade (or undoing the override write) on four writers -- a behaviour change beyond the census rows and section 0.3's bound, and it inverts D5-k. The cell does not see how to encode it inside the bound.

**Intake correction on the ruling text, encoded without a fork:** the ruling's own text contains `No swing command creates a policy.`, i.e. the substring `swing ` followed by `command`. A literal "contains no `swing ` string" assertion FAILS on the ruled text itself. Encoded as: for every registered top-level command/group name `N` of `swing.cli.main` (read at test time), `f"swing {N}"` is not in the message; `command` is not a registered name (asserted). This pins what the D35 rule means (no invocable command is named) and fails on the plan's old text (`swing config ...`).

**Done / not done:** nothing of T-D5's code is written. The ruling is block-quoted at `1b586bb7`. Parts E/F/G are unaffected (CHARC: "E/F/G do not depend on T-D5").

### STOP-4 -- three of T-G2's roster write only an audit / telemetry / cache row (a ruling that reads two ways against the code; ruler: CHARC)

**The two texts.** Plan section 8, T-G2, lists 16 web POST roots as operator-record roots, and its only move rule for that side is "Roots whose try body turns out on READ to write **nothing durable** move to T-G3". T-G3 carries CHARC's discriminator of record (R2 gate): "After a read or an **audit/cache commit**, a spurious failure report costs a retried read. After an operator-record write, the same report tells the operator the write failed and invites a retry against a durable row -- the D68 class. That is the line between DECLARED and CONVERTED." Plan section 1.2 row 5 derived the 41/17 split by COUNTING the census's own section 4.2 listing, not by applying the discriminator per root on the 41 side.

**The read (per root, what its try body commits):**
- `POST /latches/orders` (`swing/web/routes/latches.py`, `latches_orders_fragment`): its own docstring -- "It performs an AUDITED external Schwab call that inserts a `schwab_api_calls` row -- that is a write. It writes NO domain row (no `latch_view_events`, no `trades`, no `fills`)." The call runs inside `build_latch_orders_vm` -> `resolve_open_orders` (`swing/web/view_models/latches.py`).
- `POST /latches/view` (`latches_view_beacon`): `record_view` rows into `latch_view_events` under `with conn:`; the handler's own comment calls the beacon "an OBSERVER" (telemetry; a schema rejection degrades to a 409, never a 500).
- `POST /dashboard/weather-chart/refresh` (`swing/web/routes/dashboard.py`): `refresh_chart_render(conn, chart_render)` under `with conn:` -- a `chart_renders` CACHE row (DELETE-then-INSERT of the benchmark's market_weather render).
- Already CONVERTED under T-G1 before this was seen, same class: CLI `finviz fetch` (`69e11aa3`) -- its durable writes are a `finviz_api_calls` AUDIT row and the emitted screen CSV in the finviz inbox (pipeline input).

**Branch (a) -- CONVERT (the plan's roster and move rule, literally).** They write something durable, so they stay in the 41: same shape as the 12 (`durable` at the writer's return; `close_after_commit` after it; `/latches/orders` renders a body, so the notice is appended there). Cost: three more G2 rows; a close failure there becomes the route's own success plus one ERROR record. No behaviour change on any path without a close failure. `finviz fetch` stays as committed.

**Branch (b) -- DECLARE (the discriminator of record).** The three move to T-G3 with this read cited; no code change on them (a close failure stays a 500, which after an audit/telemetry/cache commit costs a retried read). `finviz fetch`'s T-G1 conversion would then be reverted (or kept, if the emitted CSV is ruled an operator record) -- one more commit on the branch.

**What is done and what waits.** The 25 CLI roots (T-G1) and the other 12 web roots (T-G2, `86bc327e`) are committed; their classification is not in question. Nothing on the three web roots is written. T-G3, T-H1, T-Z1, G2 and Reviewer A wait on this ruling (T-G3's count depends on it: 17, or 20).

### STOP-5 -- T-G3's per-root READ finds TWO roots that commit an EXEMPLAR LABEL (an operator-record class the STOP-4 rule names) and ONE that reads two ways (ruler: CHARC)

**The rule applied** (STOP-4 ruling, DECLARED-ROOT RULE): "a root is CONVERTED only if its try body commits an OPERATOR RECORD -- a row the operator authored or is accountable for (... exemplar labels ...) -- such that a false 'it failed' invites a retry against a durable row ... a T-G3 root found to write an operator record moves to the converted set." Dispatch step 5: if that happens, STOP before converting.

**The read (what each root's try body commits):**
- `patterns label-exemplars` (`swing/cli.py:6335-6580`, `label_exemplars_cmd`; opener `swing.data.db.connect`, function-local `as _connect` at `:6375`): Step C calls `fire_claude_silver_label` (`swing/patterns/labeling.py:293`), which commits `with conn: return exemplars_repo.insert_exemplar(conn, exemplar)` (`labeling.py:398-399`) -- ONE new `pattern_exemplars` row (`label_source='claude_silver'`). **Retry fact:** `pattern_exemplars` has NO unique constraint (its only indexes, `ix_pattern_exemplars_proposed_class_source` and `ix_pattern_exemplars_ticker_start_date`, are non-unique: `0020_phase13_charts_patterns_autofill_usability.sql:166` and `tests/data/schema_manifest_head.tsv`), so a retry after a false failure INSERTS A DUPLICATE silver exemplar -- the D68 shape exactly.
- `patterns review-silver-with-codex` (`swing/cli.py:6814-7017`, `review_silver_with_codex_cmd`; opener `swing.data.db.connect` as `_connect`, `:6842`): calls `fire_codex_review_for_silver_row` (`labeling.py:469`), which commits `with conn:` an `UPDATE pattern_exemplars SET codex_reviewed = 1, codex_agreement = ?` on the parent (`labeling.py:521-530`) and, on disagreement, INSERTs a second `label_source='codex_silver'` row (`labeling.py:534-590`). **Retry fact:** nothing refuses a second review of an already-reviewed parent (the guards at `labeling.py:507-516` check existence and `label_source` only), so a retry on a disagreement INSERTS A SECOND codex_silver row. (The forced RNG at `cli.py:6961-6984` guarantees the review fires, so the service's not-fired `return None` is unreachable from this root.)
- `patterns-exemplars-backfill-labeler-evidence` (`swing/cli.py:7220-7239`; opener `swing.data.db.connect`): `patterns_exemplars_backfill_labeler_evidence_run` (`cli.py:7143-7217`) commits `with conn: update_exemplar_labeler_evidence_json(...)` PER ROW (`:7205-7208`) -- it writes an exemplar row's `labeler_evidence_json`. **Reads two ways:** the TABLE is the exemplar-label table, but the write is a mechanical synthesis of derived keys (`rule_criteria`, `narrative`) and is idempotent BY MECHANISM -- an already-augmented row is skipped (`cli.py:7180-7183`, "Already augmented; idempotent skip"), so a retry is a no-op on every row the first run committed.

**Branch (a) -- MOVE to the converted set.** `label-exemplars` and `review-silver-with-codex` convert in the T-G1 shape (one CLI root-table row each, G1-a/G1-b/G1-c): `durable` bound at the service's OWN return (`fire_claude_silver_label` / `fire_codex_review_for_silver_row` each commit inside their own `with conn:`, so the return IS the durable point), `pending_refusal` = the in-flight `ClickException` (both roots raise their refusals -- shape-invalid `ValueError` -> `ClickException`, not-found -- BEFORE the durable point, so G1-b is the refusal-plus-close-failure chain with nothing written), close through `_close_after_writes`. Sub-branch for backfill: (a-i) move it too (`durable = augmented > 0` at the runner's return) -> converted 40 = 27 CLI + 13 web, T-G3 18; or (a-ii) DECLARE it with `cli.py:7180-7183` cited as the retry-harmless fact (the finviz precedent) -> converted 39 = 26 CLI + 13 web, T-G3 19.

**Branch (b) -- DECLARE all three.** Read the rule's "authored or accountable for" as excluding AI-labeler / Codex rows (a subagent authors the label; the operator couriers the response file), and cite the reads above. Converted stays 37; T-G3 stays 21; no code. Cost: a close failure after the INSERT stays a traceback over a durable row that a retry DUPLICATES (no unique constraint) -- the case the rule's "such that" clause describes.

**The cell's reading, for the ruler:** the rule's list names "exemplar labels" with no authorship carve-out, and the retry facts for the first two are measured (no unique constraint; no re-review guard), so they read as (a); backfill reads as (a-ii) by the rule's own retry test, the finviz precedent. Not decided here.

**Also read, DECLARED by the rule's letter, fact surfaced for the ruler:** `swing eval` (`cli.py:519-598`) commits `insert_evaluation_run` + `insert_candidates` in `_persist`'s `with conn:` (`:567-576`). Not in the rule's operator-record list (machine computation over a CSV; no operator authorship). Fact: both tables are IMMUTABLE by trigger (`trg_er_no_update` / `trg_er_no_delete` / `trg_er_no_replace`, `trg_candidates_no_*`, in the head manifest) and are the substrate the latch / cohort readers key on, so a retry after a false failure appends a SECOND immutable evaluation run over the same data. Encoded as DECLARED unless the ruler moves it.

**What is done and what waits.** Committed: the STOP-4 ruling (`96f10feb`), the finviz-only revert (`7515103a`), the T-G2 remainder read (the one remaining root is `/trades/{id}/exit`, converted at T-A7), and the T-G3 per-root reads (section "T-G3" below: 18 confirmed DECLARED, 3 routed here). Nothing of branch (a) is written. Waiting on this ruling: the three roots' disposition; then plan section 1.2 row 5's replacement (its numbers depend on it: 37/21, 39/19 or 40/18), T-H1, T-Z1, G2, Reviewer A.

### Persisted members with NO write-side quote -> FND-3 (by name)

- `data.models._require_utc_conversion` (called at `swing/data/models.py:3499`, `:3502` by `ProvenanceCorrection.__post_init__`, a row-built object). It checks that each `_utc` column IS the UTC conversion of its `_raw` sibling (`models.py:3191`). The HEAD DDL of `provenance_corrections` checks only each column's SHAPE (`0039_provenance_corrections_tier2.sql:293-309`, GLOB + `datetime()` round-trip) and ordering within one clock domain. No CHECK or trigger recomputes the zone conversion; SQLite has no zone database. Search: `grep -n "_utc" swing/data/migrations/0039_provenance_corrections_tier2.sql`, with each hit read. The hits are the shape CHECKs (`:293-327`), the orderings (`:406`, `:412`, `:479-482`) and the immutability trigger (`:730-731`); none of them is a conversion.
- `latches.models._require_positive_int` (called by `FireRow.__post_init__` at `swing/latches/models.py:77-88`, built from a `candidates` row at `swing/latches/reader.py:99` `load_fire_rows`, and `EntryRecord.__post_init__` `:187`/`:194`, built from a `trades` row at `reader.py:170`). The values are `candidates.id` / `trades.id` / `evaluation_run_id`, `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` / `INTEGER NOT NULL` with no `CHECK (x > 0)`. Search: the regex `CHECK \((id|candidate_id|evaluation_run_id|trade_id|pipeline_run_id) *> *0\)` over the `sqlite_master` DDL of `candidates`, `trades`, `evaluation_runs` and `pipeline_runs` in the HEAD schema built by `ensure_schema`. It found nothing; each `id` is a bare `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`. The same regex over `swing/data/migrations/*.sql` DOES find `CHECK (evaluation_run_id > 0)` at `0032:62`, `0033:78` and `0033:714`. Those constrain the `latch_view_events` and `latch_order_intents` tables, which are not where these values are read from. (Correction: the first draft of this bullet said the migration grep returned nothing before it had been run. Running it showed those three hits.)

Notes on three classifications: `data.repos.trades._validate_chart_pattern_invariant` is the WRITE barrier itself: `insert_trade_with_event` calls it at `repos/trades.py:346` on a Trade built by the service (`entry.py:2190`), not read from a row. Its docstring (`:283-288`) says the cross-column invariant has no SQL CHECK. It is `computed` by the plan's rule. `latches.identity._require_positive_int` is a different function from `latches.models._require_positive_int`; it has the same name, and its only callers are `LatchIdentity.__post_init__` (`identity.py:58-64`, built by the latch fold at `service.py:868`). `latches.models._require_finite_number` validates `DailyBar` (OHLCV archive parquet at `reader.py:723`), `Latch` (the fold) and `RestingOrder` (Schwab orders at `orders.py:137`). None of the three is a DB row.

### STOP-6 -- the self-sweep's SS-9: the T-G0 close helpers contain an interrupt, which CHARC's Round 2 class rule forbids at a cleanup statement (a ruling that reads two ways against the code; ruler: CHARC)

**Fact (measured by execution, above).** `_close_after_writes` (`swing/cli.py`) and `close_after_commit` (`swing/web/routes/__init__.py`) catch `BaseException` at the close ("the CLASS"). An interrupt at the close is SWALLOWED after a durable write (the WARN / the notice, a normal exit) and RETYPED when a refusal is pending (a `ClickException` carrying the interrupt as text, `__cause__` the interrupt). The Round 2 class rule reads: an interrupt at a cleanup statement is "logged against the in-flight error and re-raised CHAINED from it -- never swallowed, never retyped". The fix diffs route NEW closes through these helpers by ruling (R2-01 (i) and (ii), R2-06, R2-07, R2-04), so the sweep reaches them; but the helpers are the cited S-B02 / S-CONTAIN shapes serving EVERY converted root (T-G0, T-G1, T-G2), so any change is helper-wide, not "narrowly per site". Not fixed.

**Branches (both executable):**
- **(a) DECLARE.** The helpers stay. One AL line: after a durable write an interrupt at the close is contained (the WARN / notice and a normal exit), and with a refusal pending it is carried inside the refusal's `ClickException`, chained `from` the interrupt. Reason: D68 -- reporting an interrupt as the command's failure over a durable write invites the retry against it; and the class rule's words "logged against the IN-FLIGHT error" scope it to a cleanup with an error in flight, which the durable branch does not have.
- **(b) AMEND both helpers, one edit each.** Split the handler: `except Exception` keeps today's containment exactly; `except BaseException` (an interrupt) -> with a refusal pending: record, then re-raise the interrupt `from pending_refusal` (never retyped); durable with no refusal: record and emit the WARN / notice line (the write IS recorded), then re-raise the interrupt (never swallowed). Tests: each helper, an interrupt at the close, per branch (unit), plus one converted root per helper. Files: `swing/cli.py`, `swing/web/routes/__init__.py` (both in the envelope). Every converted root inherits the change.
- **(c) SPLIT.** (b) for the refusal branch only (the retype is what the rule's letter forbids, and there IS an in-flight error there); (a) for the durable branch.

**Where the cell stopped.** The Round 2 fixes and the in-ruling sweep fixes are committed; the ledger is committed. NOT run: the census line-pin re-anchor, the pre-R3 full fast suite, and Reviewer A Round 3 -- they follow the ruling (a Round 3 on a helper that the ruling may change would review a moving artifact).

> READ at this seat on 22-f-exec @ 0be9a9c8: cli.py `_close_after_writes` -- `except BaseException`; with a refusal pending it raises a ClickException built from the refusal text plus the close error's text `from close_exc` (an interrupt is RETYPED into a click error); when durable with no refusal it logs, echoes the WARN and RETURNS (an interrupt is SWALLOWED and the process continues past the operator's Ctrl-C). web/routes/__init__.py `close_after_commit` -- `except BaseException`, logs, returns the notice; the docstring says "NEVER raises -- an interrupt at the close included". cli.py:469 `migrated = version > pre_version`, bound into both tail closes at :518/:526. Matches your facts.
>
> ITEM 1 (SS-9) -- RULED: (b), BOTH branches, helper-wide. (c) is REFUSED on a misreading of my own rule that I will correct here rather than let stand: "logged against the IN-FLIGHT error and re-raised CHAINED from it" was written for the case with an error in flight; it does not say an interrupt with NOTHING in flight may be swallowed. The operative half is "never swallowed, never retyped" -- an interrupt is a SIGNAL FROM THE OPERATOR, not an error to be dispositioned, and the durable branch's WARN-and-return means Ctrl-C at the close leaves the process running to exit 0 as if nothing happened. That is worse than the refusal branch's retype, not better. Where there is nothing in flight, the interrupt is re-raised BARE (no `from`); where a refusal is pending, it is re-raised `from` the refusal (the refusal's text is thereby preserved in the chain and in the record). So:
> - `_close_after_writes`: `except Exception` keeps today's three-branch behaviour exactly. A NEW `except BaseException` (the non-Exception remainder: interrupts, SystemExit, GeneratorExit) does: refusal pending -> one contained record naming the refusal text and the interrupt, then `raise interrupt from pending_refusal`; durable -> the ERROR record and the WARN line (the durable fact IS still stated -- the operator who pressed Ctrl-C still learns the write is recorded), then `raise` the interrupt bare; nothing durable and no refusal is the byte-unchanged bare close already. The click runtime turns a propagated KeyboardInterrupt into its own "Aborted!" exit, which is the honest outcome.
> - `close_after_commit`: `except Exception` unchanged (log, return the notice); a NEW `except BaseException` logs the same record with the interrupt named, then re-raises bare -- the server's own interrupt/cancellation handling owns it from there. The docstring sentence "NEVER raises -- an interrupt at the close included" is DELETED by replacement, not annotated.
> - Helper-wide is CORRECT here, not a violation of "narrow per site": the helpers are the arc's own T-G0 code, and the roots inherit the helper's behaviour by construction -- that is what a helper is for. Tests: one unit test per branch per helper (an interrupt planted at `close()` in each of the three states), asserting the record, the WARN/notice where due, the re-raise and its `__cause__`; plus one converted root per helper driven through `patch_conn_opener` with a proxy whose `close()` raises KeyboardInterrupt, asserting the did-it-fire count and that the process does NOT continue past the close (CLI: the trailing `click.echo` after the helper is not reached; web: the route's success body is not returned). D68's reason ("a failure exit invites a retry") does not reach interrupts: the operator who interrupted is not misled about a failure; they asked for a stop.
>
> ITEM 2 -- CONFIRMED: `durable = migrated` (version > pre_version) is the correct encoding and my literal True was the R1-05 false-claim class in my own ruling text -- the third time this week a ruled text named a state the code does not measure, and the cell caught it. Keep the pin (a db-migrate at HEAD with a failing close: a plain close, no "IS recorded").
>
> SS-5 accepted as outside the class (a query is not a cleanup statement).
>

Consequence, by the ruling's design: when a NON-refusal exception is already propagating through such a `finally` and the helper runs with `durable=True`, an interrupt at the close now REPLACES that exception (it survives only as the interrupt's implicit `__context__`); before STOP-6 the helper contained the interrupt and the in-flight exception propagated. > Defect: STOP-6 remains incomplete when an ordinary close failure is followed by an interrupt from the logging cleanup. `log_contained` catches `BaseException`; `_close_after_writes` treats the returned interrupt as diagnostic text, while `close_after_commit` ignores it. Both helpers then return normally.
>
> Concrete trigger: after a durable write, make `conn.close()` raise `RuntimeError` and install a logging handler whose `emit()` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`. The CLI exits successfully with warnings; the web helper returns its success notice. The operator’s stop signal is swallowed.
>
> Fix: after attempting the remaining independent warning work, distinguish returned `Exception` failures from non-`Exception` interrupts and `raise log_interrupt from close_exc`. Add this double-failure case to both helper test modules.
>
> R3-02 — major — [swing/cli.py:377](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:377), [swing/data/db.py:609](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/data/db.py:609)
>
> REOPENS R2-03. The B-04 boundary catches every `BaseException`, including an interrupt raised by a post-commit PRAGMA restore. When the witness sees the target schema version, it converts that interrupt into the normal warning path, continues ratification and the rest of the tail, and exits zero. The local R2-03 bare re-raise is therefore neutralized one frame outward.
>
> Concrete trigger: migrate from `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION - 1`, commit the migration, then have the first restore at line 613 raise `KeyboardInterrupt`. The target-version witness succeeds, and `db-migrate` continues rather than stopping.
>
> Fix: reserve the existing recovery path for `Exception`. For a non-`Exception`, optionally perform the read-only durability witness and emit the durable warning, but re-raise the original interrupt before ratification or any remaining tail work. Add a CLI-level restore-interrupt discriminator.
>
> R3-03 — major — [swing/cli.py:1089](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:1089), [swing/cli.py:2089](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:2089), [swing/cli.py:2205](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:2205)
>
> The bespoke close handlers for `trade entry`, `trade review`, and `trade assign-intent` still catch `BaseException` using the pre-STOP-6 behavior. On a durable result they log and continue to exit zero; with a pending refusal they replace the interrupt with a `ClickException`. The outer durable-result guards at [swing/cli.py:1196](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:1196) and [swing/cli.py:2290](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:2290) would also contain a re-raised interrupt.
>
> Concrete trigger: make `close()` raise `KeyboardInterrupt` after a successful entry, review, or admitted assignment; the command completes normally. On a duplicate-entry or refused-assignment path, the interrupt becomes an exit-1 ClickException.
>
> Fix: split ordinary close exceptions from non-`Exception` interrupts in all three handlers, and ensure the outer guards re-raise cleanup interrupts instead of applying post-durability containment. With a pending refusal, raise the interrupt from that refusal. Add direct close-interrupt tests for the durable and refusal states of each bespoke handler.
>
> R3-04 — major — [swing/web/routes/trades.py:2238](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/web/routes/trades.py:2238), [swing/web/routes/trades.py:2386](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/web/routes/trades.py:2386), [swing/web/routes/trades.py:3193](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/web/routes/trades.py:3193), [swing/web/routes/trades.py:3254](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/web/routes/trades.py:3254)
>
> The entry and exit POST routes retain the old S-CONTAIN treatment for every `BaseException`. A close-time `KeyboardInterrupt` after the write commits is logged and swallowed, after which a success response is rendered. Even if the inner close handler re-raised it, the outer `except BaseException` would convert it into a degraded-success response.
>
> Concrete trigger: successfully record an entry or exit using a connection whose real close completes and then raises `KeyboardInterrupt`. The route proceeds to its render path and returns HTTP 200 instead of propagating the stop signal.
>
> Fix: separate ordinary post-commit failures from non-`Exception` interrupts in both the close and outer guards. Record the durable fact, then re-raise the interrupt bare. Add direct-route tests with the first renderer after the close wrapped by a counter, mirroring the watchlist STOP-6 test.
>
> R3-05 — major — [OUT-OF-ENVELOPE] — [swing/cli.py:2531](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:2531)
>
> The D69 `trade backfill-intent` close guard also preserves the old behavior. With no error in flight, a close interrupt is converted into warnings and a normal return. With an error in flight, it logs the interrupt against that error but then lets the original error resume, swallowing the interrupt rather than raising it chained from the original.
>
> Concrete trigger: write at least one row, then have `close()` raise `KeyboardInterrupt`, either normally or while a later prompt/refusal is already propagating.
>
> Fix: contain only ordinary close exceptions. For an interrupt, attempt the independent record/warning work and then raise it bare, or `from _exc_in_flight` when one exists.
>
> Summary: The typed translations, writer-reported durability bindings, and rollback/savepoint fixes inspected appear intact, but STOP-6 is not complete. Five major interrupt-hygiene defects remain: secondary logging interrupts are swallowed, `db-migrate` catches a re-raised restore interrupt, four in-envelope bespoke CLI/web close shapes retain the old containment semantics, and the explicitly out-of-envelope D69 root has the same defect. This was a read-only review; no tests were run.
>
> NEW_CRITICAL_MAJOR_FOUND
>

#### Codex response (verbatim final message)
> R3-06 — major — `swing/data/db.py:1134` — **REOPENS R2-08(b).** `ensure_schema` still catches only `Exception`, although this arc widened `_apply_migration` to propagate interrupts. Consequently, a `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit`, or `GeneratorExit` from the migration, rollback, or PRAGMA restore bypasses the migration-error cleanup entirely; `ensure_schema` neither calls `conn.close()` nor returns the owned connection. An interrupted rollback can therefore leave an active migration transaction and write lock reachable through the exception traceback. Concrete trigger: make `executescript` open a transaction and fail, then make `rollback()` raise `KeyboardInterrupt`; the interrupt escapes with the correct cause, but the connection’s close count remains zero. Fix: widen the handler to `except BaseException as migration_error`; the existing split close handlers already provide the required containment/chaining. Extend the C2-21-b/R2-08(b) tests to assert that close fires for an interrupt originating in the migration, including the interrupted-rollback posture.
>
> R3-07 — minor — `swing/trades/schwab_reconciliation.py:1419` — **REOPENS R2-05.** The fresh fallback savepoint’s `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` remains under an `except Exception` only. If an interrupt arrives there while the preceding validator/dispatch rejection `e` is being handled, it bypasses the local handler. The run-level handler rolls back the whole run and re-raises the interrupt, protecting data, but the interrupt has no ERROR record and `__cause__` remains unset instead of being chained from `e`—the same defect corrected at the other rollback-following RELEASE sites by SS-3/SS-4. Concrete trigger: drive the auto-redirect decline path, allow the fallback ambiguity stamp to succeed, and have `RELEASE SAVEPOINT correction_fallback_sp_*` raise `KeyboardInterrupt`. Fix: guard that RELEASE specifically, preserving the current `sqlite3.Error` path while recording a non-`Exception` interrupt and raising it `from e`; add a did-it-fire discriminator for this third RELEASE site.
>
> The close helpers, STOP-7 propagation, exit POST guards, durability reports, and the remaining reviewed transaction templates conform to the recorded rulings. The incomplete R2-08(b) cleanup is a new major finding; the savepoint issue is interrupt-only and data-safe because the outer rollback still executes.
>
> NEW_CRITICAL_MAJOR_FOUND

#### Proposed dispositions (cell 13; NOTHING fixed -- the orchestrator's reply decides; no fourth counted round is authorized)
Each finding's load-bearing fact was READ on disk at `edd61c20`. Provenance was established with `git blame -L n,n --porcelain` plus `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> 704ac133`, and the base shape with `git show 704ac133:<file>`.

| id | sev (Codex) | fact read | provenance | proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R3-06 | major, REOPENS R2-08(b) | `ensure_schema` (`db.py:1130-1155`): `run_migrations(...)` sits under `except Exception as migration_error:`. That handler holds the R2-08(b) close guard, which contains an `Exception` from the close and chains an interrupt from it. A `BaseException` that is not an `Exception`, raised by `run_migrations` (C2-21-b / R2-03 now re-raise interrupts out of `_apply_migration` after attempting the rollback and the restores), therefore skips `conn.close()`. If the rollback itself was interrupted, the connection still holds an open write transaction until the traceback is dropped. The SOLE production caller is `db_migrate` (`cli.py:376`; `grep -rn "ensure_schema(" swing/`). Its B-04 `except BaseException` runs the read-only witness (below target -> bare re-raise; at target -> WARN, then R3-02's bare re-raise), click then gives `Aborted!`, and the process exits | the trigger class is PRE-ARC: at `704ac133` the handler is `except Exception: conn.close(); raise`. The line itself blames to this arc's `f336e4db` (R2-08(b) rewrote it as `except Exception as migration_error:`), and the R2-08(b) encoding in the plan names the handler as `except Exception`. Pre-arc, an interrupt in the migration got NO rollback at all (`_apply_migration`'s `except Exception`); C2-21-b added the rollback | **ROUTE to CHARC**, because it changes a ruled encoding (R2-08(b)). The two branches: **(a) FIX**: widen to `except BaseException as migration_error:`, a one-token change. The existing split close guard then contains a close `Exception` as a NOTE on the interrupt and chains a close interrupt from it, and the bare `raise` re-raises the migration's interrupt. > ## Claim disposition
>
> | Claim | Result |
> |---|---|
> | C1 | No defect found in the typed CLI/web translations reviewed. |
> | C2 | Pass. All Part-F `BEGIN` sites have the required `BaseException`/conditional-rollback/chained-cleanup shape. |
> | C3 | Fail: the three Schwab auth roots and their tests treat an audit-only commit as sufficient durability evidence. |
> | C4 | Fail: two CLI cleanup paths mishandle interrupts. |
> | C5 | Pass. The interrupted migration rollback escaped with its cause, and `ensure_schema` closed exactly once in the in-memory execution. |
> | C6 | No defect found in the bounded amendments or new types. |
> | C7 | Fail: the Schwab refusal stubs lack the required did-it-fire assertion; their wrappers are discarded. |
> | C8 | Pass against the supplied diff: no schema, dependency, new `swing/` module, or carve-out violation found. |
>
> ## Findings
>
> 1. **Major — INTRODUCED — demonstrated by execution:** the CLI helper bypasses its interrupt handler on the nothing-durable/no-refusal branch.
>
>    At [swing/cli.py:1338](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:1338), `conn.close()` executes before the helper’s `try`. When a writer’s unexpected `RuntimeError` is propagating with `durable=False`, and `close()` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`, the interrupt is neither recorded nor explicitly chained from the writer error.
>
>    I executed the exact helper definition with an in-memory connection proxy. The result was:
>
>    ```text
>    KeyboardInterrupt cause=None context=RuntimeError('writer failed') close_calls=1
>    ```
>
>    This contradicts C4’s requirement that an interrupt during cleanup be recorded and raised `from` the in-flight error. The item-4 test misses the branch by forcing `durable=True` at [test_22f_close_after_writes_helper.py:256](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/tests/cli/test_22f_close_after_writes_helper.py:256).
>
> 2. **Major — INTRODUCED — demonstrated by reading:** the new trade-entry refusal branch retypes a close interrupt as `ClickException`.
>
>    At [swing/cli.py:1106](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli.py:1106), the handler catches all `BaseException`; added lines 1114–1119 then construct a new `ClickException` whenever a typed refusal is in flight, regardless of whether the close error is an ordinary `Exception` or `KeyboardInterrupt`.
>
>    Concrete scenario: a duplicate-position refusal is propagating and `conn.close()` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`. Instead of propagating the interrupt `from` the pending refusal, the command raises a `ClickException` from the interrupt. The stop signal is retyped, and the chain direction is reversed.
>
>    This challenges the “pre-arc bespoke close handler” banking: the broad handler is pre-arc, but the defective interrupt-routing branch is newly added by this arc, so the default-to-introduced rule applies.
>
> 3. **Major — INTRODUCED — demonstrated by reading:** the Schwab setup, refresh, and logout roots do not bind durability from an operator-record report, and their acceptance test explicitly uses only an audit row.
>
>    The roots assign `durable = True` merely after reaching the line at [cli_schwab.py:218](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli_schwab.py:218), [cli_schwab.py:330](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli_schwab.py:330), and [cli_schwab.py:410](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/cli_schwab.py:410).
>
>    Worse, the G1 test stub commits only a `schwab_api_calls` row at [test_22f_close_after_durable.py:432](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/tests/cli/test_22f_close_after_durable.py:432), and its probe counts only that audit table. The parametrized test then requires the “write IS recorded” warning and exit 0. This is the exact state C3/STOP-4 says is not durable: an audit row is not an operator record.
>
>    The real services currently perform external token/config actions on successful returns, but this implementation and test do not establish that fact from the writer’s report and would bless an audit-only normal return.
>
> 4. **Minor — INTRODUCED — demonstrated by reading:** the Schwab refusal tests violate the did-it-fire rule.
>
>    `_refusing_service` creates a counting `raising(...)` wrapper but discards it at [test_22f_close_after_durable.py:467](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/tests/cli/test_22f_close_after_durable.py:467). The G1-b test at [test_22f_close_after_durable.py:916](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/tests/cli/test_22f_close_after_durable.py:916) asserts the close proxy fired but never asserts the planted writer fired. A missed writer patch could therefore fall into a real Schwab authentication failure with similar operator text without failing on the required seam assertion.
>

> - Exercised the CLI close helper’s missing cross-product described in finding 1.

#### Codex response (verbatim final message, `codex-review-r4-attempt1.txt:43156-43188`)
> ### R4-01 — major — INTRODUCED
>
> **File:** `swing/trades/schwab_reconciliation.py:2924-2927`
>
> **Defect:** The run-failure preservation handler violates the interrupt class rule. If `update_run_failed()` or its `commit()` raises a non-`Exception`, it first rolls back using the original reconciliation error as provenance, then bare-raises the interrupt. With a successful rollback, the interrupt has no explicit cause and no record naming the original error. If rollback also fails, `_rollback_run_or_raise(conn, exc)` chains the rollback failure directly from the original error, omitting the intervening preservation interrupt from both the explicit chain and record.
>
> **Trigger:** A discrepancy operation raises an ordinary error after `run_id` exists; while the handler is preserving the failed run, `update_run_failed()` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`. With a working rollback, the interrupt escapes with `__cause__ is None`. With a failing rollback, the rollback error is chained from the discrepancy error rather than the cleanup interrupt.
>
> **Fix:** Split the non-`Exception` preservation branch. Attempt rollback, but log one record naming the original failure and preservation interrupt, then raise the interrupt `from exc`. If rollback also fails, preserve all three layers by raising the rollback failure from the preservation interrupt, with that interrupt chained from the original. Add both successful-rollback and failed-rollback interrupt discriminators; the existing both-verbs-fail test does not distinguish the original error from the preservation error.
>
> ### R4-02 — major — PRE-ARC by line, still unhandled by this arc
>
> **File:** `swing/trades/reconciliation.py:481-498`, reached through the arc-modified handler at `swing/trades/reconciliation.py:631-640`
>
> **Defect:** `run_tos_reconciliation` has the same missing handler-cleanup interrupt discipline. While the original `reconcile_tos` exception is in flight, `update_run_failed()` and its `commit()` are recovery operations. An interrupt there reaches the outer `except BaseException`, which rolls back and bare-raises it without a record and without `raise interrupt from original`. The ledger’s T-F3 reason that this inner block “is not a rollback site” does not hold under CHARC’s later rule, which applies to an interrupt arriving at a cleanup/recovery statement while another error is in flight.
>
> **Trigger:** `reconcile_tos()` raises an ordinary parsing or reconciliation error after the run row is inserted, followed by `KeyboardInterrupt` during `repo.update_run_failed()` or `conn.commit()`. After a successful rollback, the interrupt escapes with the reconciliation error only as implicit context and no record naming both.
>
> **Fix:** Guard the failed-run preservation sequence locally. Attempt the remaining rollback first, emit one contained record naming the original failure and interrupt, and raise the interrupt explicitly `from` the original. Add a nested-failure test; the current F2 test only interrupts the primary transaction body.
>
> ### R4-03 — major — INTRODUCED
>
> **File:** `swing/web/routes/latches.py:1314-1318`; writer contract at `swing/data/repos/latch_order_intents.py:104-154`
>
> **Defect:** `/latches/intent` sets `durable = True` merely because `record_intent()` returned, but that writer explicitly has two normal-return no-write paths: an existing-key replay and the lost-race `INSERT … DO NOTHING` path. The route-level replay checks do not close the race because the writer performs its own later SELECT. This repeats the R2-04 class: durability is not obtained from a writer report saying whether this invocation wrote.
>
> **Trigger:** Two identical POSTs both pass the route’s replay prechecks. The first commits; the second reaches `record_intent()`, returns the first request’s existing row without writing, and then its close raises. The second request incorrectly takes the durable containment branch and returns success with an “IS recorded” close notice, although retrying it would be a no-op and this invocation created or altered nothing. The G2 test only exercises a fresh insert, so it misses this path.
>
> **Fix:** Make the writer report `wrote` atomically alongside the stored row and bind `durable` from that field, as with `CorrectionResult.wrote`. Add a lost-race-plus-close-failure discriminator asserting the bare-close failure, no helper record, and no close notice. Changing the repository return contract would require an explicit envelope grant.
>
> Three major findings remain: two recovery-handler interrupt paths lose required provenance, and one converted web root cannot distinguish its writer’s committed insert from its documented no-write returns. The Reviewer B B-1 through B-4 changes themselves appear correctly wired and discriminated. Review was static as required; no tests were run.
>
> NEW_CRITICAL_MAJOR_FOUND

> ## Finding
>
> - **Major — INTRODUCED — [swing/trades/schwab_reconciliation.py:2944](/mnt/c/Users/rwsmy/swing-trading/.worktrees/22-f-exec/swing/trades/schwab_reconciliation.py:2944)**  
>   The R4-01 preserve handler masks the original run failure when `update_run_failed()` or `commit()` raises an ordinary non-`sqlite3.Error` `Exception`. After rollback succeeds, the bare `raise` at line 2944 executes inside the nested handler and re-raises `preserve_error`, instead of falling through to line 2948 where the original `exc` would propagate. This contradicts C13 and the adjacent “original exception propagates” comment.
>
>   Demonstrated against a fully migrated in-memory v41 database: a `ValueError("original run failure")` was planted after the run row, and `update_run_failed` raised `RuntimeError("ordinary preserve failure")`. Rollback completed and `in_transaction` became false, but the `RuntimeError` escaped with neither cause nor context; the original `ValueError` was completely lost. The existing R4-01 tests cover interrupts and interrupted rollback, but not this claimed ordinary-Exception preservation case.
>
>   The ordinary-Exception branch should fall through like the `sqlite3.Error` branch; only a non-`Exception` interrupt should be explicitly re-raised from `exc`.
>
> I found no other new critical or major issue. The helper fixes C9/C10, interrupted `ensure_schema` rollback, explicit-BEGIN sites, sampled CLI/web durable roots, and changed monkeypatch/proxy targets were consistent with their claims under reading and targeted in-memory execution.

#### Orchestrator reading of B2-01 (on disk; NOTHING fixed -- this goes to CHARC)

- **The code is as stated.** At `swing/trades/schwab_reconciliation.py:2944`, the bare `raise` sits inside `except BaseException as preserve_error:`. An ordinary non-`sqlite3.Error` `Exception` at the preserve write is rolled back (`:2927`) and then propagates IN PLACE OF the run's `exc`.
- **Per-line blame:** `:2944` is `e874a7b4` (T-F5, this arc). R4-01 did not touch it: cell 16's lines are `:2925-2927` and `:2929-2943`.
- **Behaviour vs the base `704ac133` (`:2785-2795` there).** The base caught only `except sqlite3.Error:`, so the SAME ordinary `Exception` escaped the handler and replaced `exc`. It did so WITHOUT any rollback, leaving the outer transaction open. T-F5 kept which exception propagates and ADDED the S-D39 rollback. So this is an arc-owned line carrying pre-arc behaviour, and it is not a regression.
- **Reachability.** The inner try holds exactly two statements. `repo.update_run_failed` (`swing/data/repos/reconciliation.py:219-239`) is ONE `conn.execute`, and `conn.commit()` follows it. Both raise only `sqlite3.Error` in the declared envelope. An ordinary `Exception` needs a planted writer, as in the reviewer's harness, or something out of envelope: `MemoryError`, or an `exc` whose `__str__` raises, which would be evaluated in the error-message f-string.
- **Demonstration caveat.** The reviewer's "neither cause nor context" came from raising through `(_ for _ in ()).throw(...)`. A generator frame starts with no handled exception, so the planted error's `__context__` is not set by the handler it escapes. Implicit context is not a record in any case (CHARC's item 4), so this does not change the substance. My plain-`raise` rerun reached a different, earlier failure on a bare `:memory:` DB and is not evidence either way.
- **Class.** This is D39 F3-shaped ("a cleanup failure that REPLACES the original error"), at the preserve WRITE rather than the rollback. It is NOT the interrupt-at-cleanup class; the interrupt branch is `:2929-2943` and is fixed. CHARC's Round 4 ruling routes a NEW class to the operator, so the class call is CHARC's to make first.
- **Proposed disposition (the orchestrator's; CHARC rules):**
  - **(a) DECLINE for this arc** as pre-arc behaviour on an arc-rewritten line, unreachable in the envelope. Bank ONE register facet: "the preserve-write handler lets an ordinary non-sqlite `Exception` replace the run's error", site `:2944`. Pass 2 then stands, dispositioned, as the merge-gate transcript.
  - **(b) Fix without a counted round:** let an ordinary `Exception` fall through so `exc` propagates, with one `log_contained_note` record naming the preserve error, RED-first. Then Reviewer B pass 3. The cost is one fix cell plus about 900K tokens for B again, to change a pre-arc behaviour the writer cannot currently reach.
  - I recommend **(a)**.
